This report presents the County and Subcounty profiles for all Districts in Kigezi Sub
Region based on the National Population and Housing Census 2024 undertaken by
the Uganda Bureau of Statistics (UBOS).
Recommended Citation
Uganda Bureau of Statistics 2025: County and Subcounty profiles – Kigezi Sub Region
– Volume 2, Kampala Uganda.

GLOSSARY OF TERMS
a) Special Age groups are defined as follows;
• Children Under-5; 0-4years
• Children; 0-17years
• Primary school-age children; 6-12years
• Secondary school-age; 13-18years
• Adults; 18years and above
• Older persons eligible for SAGE; 80years and above
• Age group International standard National context
i. Working-age population 15 years and above 14-64 years
ii. Youth 15–24 years 18–30 years
iii. Older persons 65 years and above 60 years and above.
b) Health Insurance policy is an agreement between the insured and insurance company in which
the company agrees to cover the cost of certain listed medical benefits such as testing, drugs,
and treatment services.
c) Mental health disorder (general psychological distress) is a psychiatric condition characterized
by disturbances in cognition, emotion, or behavior, leading to distress or disability in important
areas of life such as socializing, work, or self-care.
• Probable anxiety disorder: The likelihood of someone being anxious i.e., normally
feels worried, nervous or anxious.
• Probable depressive disorder: Losing interest in pleasurable activities and having
low feelings, sadness and stress.
• Probable psychosis: Seeing things that others don’t see or hearing voices that others
don’t hear
• Probable bipolar affective disorder: Having extreme emotions and mood swings
• Probable Suicidality: The experience of suicidal plans, thoughts or attempts
d) Unemployment rate is the proportion of the unemployed population to the total Labour force.
According to the 19th International Conference of Labour Statistics (ICLS) of the ILO, a person
is considered unemployed if they; a) Had no work from which they derive some income, b) Had
taken active steps to look for employment and c) Were willing to be employed if an employment
opportunity had presented itself.
e) Youth Not in Employment, Education, or Training (NEET) refers to those youth who are not
engaged in any form of employment, formal education, or vocational training.
f) Improved drinking water sources are those that, by nature of its construction or through active
intervention, is likely to be protected from outside contamination (UNICEF). Improved water
sources to include: piped water, boreholes, protected springs, and rainwater.
g) Unimproved drinking water may be contaminated with human or animal faeces containing
pathogens, or with chemical and physical contaminants with harmful effects on child health and
development. Unimproved drinking water sources included unprotected well/spring, vendor,
tanker truck, bottled water and river/lake.
h) Improved sanitation is defined as an improved toilet facility that is not shared with any other
household. Improved’ sanitation facilities are those designed to hygienically separate human
excreta from human contact. These include wet sanitation technologies such as flush and pour
flush toilets connected to sewers, septic tanks or pit latrines, sanitation technologies such as dry
pit latrines with slabs, ventilated improved pit latrines and composting toilets
i) Subsistence Economy refers to a system where households produce goods primarily for their
own consumption, with minimal or no surplus for sale or trade. Households within the
subsistence economy are those unable to satisfy their basic needs. Essential household
requirements encompass both food and non-food items.

INTRODUCTION
1.1 General Information about Uganda
Location and Size
Uganda is located in East Africa and lies across the equator, about 800 kilometers inland
from the Indian Ocean. It lies between 10 29’ South and 40 12’ North latitude, 290 34
East and 350 0’ East longitude. The country is landlocked, bordered by Kenya in the
East; South Sudan in the North; Democratic Republic of Congo in the West; Tanzania
in the South; and Rwanda in Southwest. It has a total land area of 241,553 square
kilometers.
Administration
At the time the NPHC 2024 was conducted, Uganda was divided administratively into
135 districts and 11 cities. The Census analysis is based on the 17 Sub Region
groupings as classified in the First Schedule of the 1995 Constitution of the Republic of
Uganda (Figure 1.1). The role of these Local Governments is to implement and monitor
Government programs at the respective levels. Over time, the administrative units have
been sub-divided with the aim of easing administration and improving the delivery of
services. The numbers of administrative units on the various census nights since 1969
are given in Table 1.1.
Table 1.1: Number of Administrative Units by Census, 1969 – 2024
Level of Administrative Unit 1969 1980 1991 2002 2014 2024

1.2 About the National Population and Housing Census 2024
The main objective of the NPHC 2024 was to generate benchmark statistical data to
inform planning processes at national and lower levels. The specific objectives were:
i) To ascertain size, structure and distribution of the population
ii) To gather data on housing conditions and access to basic services
iii) To monitor changes in key social and economic indicators since the previous
Census
iv) To update census maps and lists of Enumeration Areas for effective execution
of the census, construction of efficient area sampling frames for subsequent
surveys and geographical maps at the lowest level.
v) To establish the statistical infrastructure for future operations at the lowest Local
Government level.
vi) To further enhance the capacity of UBOS staff to undertake future censuses and
large-scale sample surveys.
vii) Inform policies and programs aimed at improving the quality of life of all
Ugandans.
Pre-Census Enumeration
i) The Bureau conducted countrywide geo-referenced mapping of households
between June 2022 and April 2024 in preparation for Census enumeration.
Mopping up of the geo-mapping to include villages missing in the geography by
Census time continued during the Census period, which enabled the entire
country to be geo-mapped, yielding a total of 10,806,982 households. Census
mapping resulted in delineation of administrative boundaries into manageable
units called Enumeration Areas.
ii) Development of the Census instruments through a highly consultative process
with stakeholders before the Census enumeration to discuss user needs
regarding the Census topics. The results of the consultations aided in revising
the Census questionnaires, which yielded 178 questions.
iii) Establishment of the Information, Communication and Technology Infrastructure
to support Census implementation and other post-Census statistical
undertakings in the National Statistical System.
iv) Publicity and advocacy for the National Population and Housing Census 2024.
The Census Office organized a communication and advocacy campaign to
create public awareness and knowledge that facilitated countrywide grass-roots
participation in the NPHC 2024.
v) Procurement of Census materials and logistics and delivering them in all Local
Governments.
vi) Preparing (provisioning) and deploying electronic equipment in preparation for
training and data collection.
vii) Recruitment and training of Census Enumerators and Supervisors.
viii)Deployment of Census materials and field staff to their responsibility areas.
ix) Intensive publicity and advocacy to create awareness about Census activities.

-- 15 of 334 --

Page | 6
Census Enumeration
i) Listing of all households before the main enumeration.
ii) Complete count of all households and the entire population, including floating
population, hotel population, refugee population, institutional population and
persons in transit.
iii) Collection of individual, household and community characteristics. All the data
collected from the field by enumerators was sent from the tablets directly to
UBOS hyperconverged servers, which are housed in the NITA. (U) space at
Statistics House in a tamper-proof environment. During data collection, the
external connection, mainly to the Android tablets used by enumerators, passed
through a robust firewall that ensured only the appropriate and secure data
exchange occurred. Raw data from the field was transmitted to the servers in real
time and there was a dashboard on which data transmission was being
monitored.
iv) Real-time uploading of data to the central server for processing.
v) The entire data transfer process was automated, minimizing the risk of
unintentional or intentional human errors. Hence, the system is robust and secure
for handling Census data.
vi) All the NPHC 2024 data is safely backed up at a Disaster Recovery Centre
located at a secure NITA (U) space.
vii) Technical staff access data from various workstations connected to the servers
through Stata version 18.0 software installed on the servers. These workstations
leverage the computing power of the server and are secured through
sophisticated authentication measures to restrict access to only authorized
personnel of UBOS.
viii)Retrieval and replacement of all ICT equipment that may malfunction during
enumeration.
ix) Publicity and advocacy for the National Population and Housing Census 2024.
Post-Census Enumeration
i) The Post-Enumeration Survey (PES), which was successfully conducted in
August 2024, by independent experts, to validate the content and coverage of
the Census enumeration. The PES established an undercount of 4.3%,
translating into 1,973,933 persons not counted. These will be incorporated in the
population projections going forward.
ii) Retrieval of the tablets and other Census materials.
iii) Census data processing and analysis were done from the processing servers
and files saved on separate folders on the storage server within the
hyperconverged environment.

-- 16 of 334 --

Page | 7
iv) Release of preliminary estimates of population counts at district level. His
Excellency the President launched the Preliminary Report on 27th June 2024.
The report provided initial insights into the country’s population size, population
distribution by age group disaggregated by sex, and population up to district/city
level.
v) Release of provisional results by socio-economic characteristics at district level.
On 3rd October 2024, the Provisional Report with more details on demographic
and socio-economic data was launched by the Rt. Honorable Prime Minister.
That report had a clerical error on tribe/ethnic population for figures from the 2014
Census, which generated heated debate and public outrage.
vi) Release of the final report on 31st December 2024.
vii) Dissemination of subject-specific thematic Census reports, policy briefs and area
maps, among others, by June 2025.
1.3 The Census 2024 Final Results
The total population of Uganda was 45,905,417 persons with an average annual growth
rate of 2.9 percent as shown in Table 1.3. More findings from the Census can be found
in the NPHC 2024 Final Report – Volume 1 (Main) on the UBOS website
(https://www.ubos.org).
